\chapter{Assignment 6: EDA and AI
Audit}\label{assignment-6-eda-and-ai-audit}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-8}

This assignment builds a transparent preliminary analysis. You will
define a cohort, check missingness, create a Table 1-style descriptive
summary, audit planted errors, and revise a provenance/stewardship note
for reuse in M2.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-7}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Define an analysis cohort before summarizing results.
\item
  Report missingness for key variables.
\item
  Create a Table 1-style summary with N, mean, and SD.
\item
  Detect planted correctness, reproducibility, interpretation, and
  stewardship errors.
\item
  Write descriptive methods text that avoids causal overclaiming.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-7}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Dataset: \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}
\item
  Worked example:
  \texttt{weeks/week07-eda-ai-audit/eda03-worked-example.qmd}
\item
  Studio activity:
  \texttt{weeks/week07-eda-ai-audit/eda06-studio-planted-errors.qmd}
\item
  Walkthrough and reference:
  \texttt{weeks/week07-eda-ai-audit/eda07-assignment-a6-and-reference.qmd}
\item
  Planted-error starter in your personal workspace:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment06-eda-ai-audit/planted-error-starter.qmd}
\item
  Audit template in your personal workspace:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment06-eda-ai-audit/audit-note-template.md}
\item
  M2 connection: \texttt{milestones/m2-preliminary-analysis/README.md}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-5}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Create or use the folder
  \texttt{assignments/assignment06-eda-ai-audit/submission/}.
\item
  Load the NHANES CSV snapshot with a relative path.
\item
  Define and document an analysis cohort using age 20-80.
\item
  Create a missingness table for key variables.
\item
  Produce a Table 1-style summary by \texttt{IncomeGroup}.
\item
  Include N, BMI mean (SD), and age mean (SD).
\item
  Write a short methods note stating that the analysis is descriptive,
  unweighted, and non-causal.
\item
  Audit the planted-error starter and list at least five issues.
\item
  Categorize each issue as correctness, reproducibility, interpretation,
  or stewardship.
\item
  Revise the provenance/stewardship statement so it names the data
  source, classroom-use context, and one privacy or stewardship caution.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-5}

AI may help draft code, explain an error, or improve the organization of
the audit note. You must verify cohort filters, missingness handling,
labels, summary statistics, and interpretation against the code and
output. Include \texttt{ai-use-note.md}.

\texttt{ai-use-note.md} is the graded AI artifact; the inline
\texttt{\#\ AI\ prompt:} / \texttt{\#\ Verified:} comments in your code
are the required working practice that feeds it --- copy your comment
pairs into the note. The template and a filled example are on the
\hyperref[the-official-ai-documentation-rule]{assignments index}.

\section{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-5}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The EDA note must render in GitHub Codespaces.
\item
  Paths must be relative.
\item
  \texttt{table1.csv} must be generated by code.
\item
  Missingness handling must be stated before interpreting summaries.
\item
  The methods note must avoid causal and population-level claims
  unsupported by the unweighted classroom example.
\end{itemize}

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-5}

Place all submission files in:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{assignments/assignment06{-}eda{-}ai{-}audit/submission/}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Submit these files:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{eda-note.qmd}: rerunnable Quarto file with cohort definition,
  missingness table, Table 1, and methods note.
\item
  \texttt{eda-note.html}: rendered output.
\item
  \texttt{table1.csv}: exported Table 1 or equivalent descriptive
  summary.
\item
  \texttt{audit-note.md}: at least five planted issues with category,
  why it matters, and corrected approach.
\item
  \texttt{provenance-stewardship-note.md}: short revised note suitable
  for reuse in M2.
\item
  \texttt{ai-use-note.md}: what AI helped with and how you checked it,
  or a sentence saying AI was not used.
\end{itemize}

\section{Submission Route}\label{submission-route-4}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the required files.

\section{Grading Checklist}\label{grading-checklist-5}

Canvas applies a 7-point rubric. This assignment is part of the
best-4-of-6 set for Assignments 4-9:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.4286}}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5714}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Criterion
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft
Points
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Cohort uses the required age 20-80 filter and exclusions are documented
before results & 1 \\
Missingness table is complete for key variables & 1 \\
Table 1 includes N, BMI mean (SD), and age mean (SD) by group & 1.5 \\
Methods note is descriptive, unweighted, and non-causal & 1 \\
Audit note identifies at least five planted issues across the four
categories: correctness, reproducibility, interpretation, and
stewardship & 1.25 \\
Provenance/stewardship and AI-use notes are specific & 0.75 \\
Required files render, export, commit, and sync & 0.5 \\
\end{longtable}

The provenance/stewardship note and the AI-use note share one rubric
row, but they are two separate required files with different jobs: the
stewardship note is about the data and its responsible use, while the
AI-use note is about how you used and verified AI. Write each one fully
--- a strong AI-use note does not cover for a missing stewardship note,
or the reverse.

\section{Definition of Done}\label{definition-of-done-14}

You are done when \texttt{eda-note.qmd} renders, \texttt{table1.csv} is
regenerated by code, and the audit note explains at least five planted
errors and corrected approaches.

\chapter{Assignment 7: Dashboard KT
Prototype}\label{assignment-7-dashboard-kt-prototype}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-9}

This assignment introduces dashboard-style Knowledge Translation. You
will run or render a small dashboard product, identify the audience and
question, document one control or interaction, improve one
audience-facing element, test the product, and state a limitation.

\textbf{What you are not doing in A7:} you are not building a full Shiny
app, deploying a live dashboard, changing the dataset, or adding new
analysis. You are making a small documentation or audience-facing text
improvement to the starter dashboard. A live dashboard is not required
unless the assignment says so. A static preview, screenshot, or rendered
HTML page is acceptable when it clearly shows the KT product and passes
the privacy check.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-8}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Explain how a dashboard differs from a static report.
\item
  Identify an audience-specific KT question.
\item
  Document one control, filter, or dashboard-style interaction.
\item
  Connect one visualization to a plain-language interpretation.
\item
  Test a dashboard product in Codespaces and record the result.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-8}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Week 8 index: \texttt{weeks/week08-dashboard-kt/index.qmd}
\item
  Required core artifact in your personal workspace:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment07-dashboard/dashboard-starter.qmd}
\item
  Walkthrough and reference:
  \texttt{weeks/week08-dashboard-kt/dash07-assignment-a7-and-reference.qmd}
\item
  Shiny exemplar (instructor demonstration only; not an A7 submission
  pathway): \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/app/app.R}
\item
  Cached data in your personal workspace:
  \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.rds}
\item
  CSV snapshot in your personal workspace:
  \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}
\item
  Adaptation template:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment07-dashboard/dashboard-adaptation-template.md}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-6}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Create or use the folder
  \texttt{assignments/assignment07-dashboard/submission/}.
\item
  Copy \texttt{assignments/assignment07-dashboard/dashboard-starter.qmd}
  to
  \texttt{assignments/assignment07-dashboard/submission/dashboard.qmd}.
  Adapt and render this copy from the repository root. The Shiny
  exemplar is a demonstration and is not an approved A7 submission
  pathway without prior instructor approval.
\item
  Choose one audience, such as a public-health analyst, student project
  team, or briefing group.
\item
  Write one audience-specific dashboard question.
\item
  Identify one control, filter, or dashboard-style interaction and the
  visualization it affects.
\item
  Identify one label, caption, help text, interpretation note, or README
  sentence that could be clearer for that audience.
\item
  Make one documentation-only or UI-text-only improvement. Do not change
  analytic logic for this assignment.
\item
  Re-render the starter dashboard with a different control value and
  record the test result.
\item
  Write one limitation statement that belongs in or beside the
  dashboard.
\item
  Complete the privacy-safe publishing check before sharing screenshots
  or rendered outputs.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-6}

AI may help improve wording for labels, captions, or limitation
statements. You must verify that the revised language still matches the
dashboard output and does not imply causality, population inference, or
access to row-level data. Include \texttt{ai-use-note.md}.

\section{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-6}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The dashboard or starter dashboard must run or render in GitHub
  Codespaces.
\item
  Paths must be relative.
\item
  The testing log must name the command or render path used.
\item
  Screenshots must avoid exposing row-level data.
\item
  The submitted adaptation must not require private credentials or
  local-only files.
\end{itemize}

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-6}

Place all submission files in:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{assignments/assignment07{-}dashboard/submission/}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Submit these files:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{dashboard-adaptation-note.md} {[}required{]}: audience,
  question, control/interaction, visualization, changed element, and KT
  rationale.
\item
  \texttt{dashboard.qmd} {[}required{]}: the adapted Quarto source used
  to create the preview.
\item
  \texttt{before-after-text.md} {[}required{]} {[}brief --- 1-3 focused
  bullets or sentences are enough{]}: original text and revised text.
\item
  \texttt{testing-log.md} {[}required{]} {[}brief --- 1-3 focused
  bullets or sentences are enough{]}: command or render path used, date
  tested, and result. A few lines is enough.
\item
  \texttt{limitation-note.md} {[}required{]} {[}brief --- 1-3 focused
  bullets or sentences are enough{]}: one limitation or caution suitable
  for the dashboard. One clear sentence is enough.
\item
  \texttt{privacy-check.md} {[}required{]} {[}brief --- 1-3 focused
  bullets or sentences are enough{]}: evidence that no row-level data,
  small-cell overclaim, or causal overclaim is shared. Three short
  sentences are enough.
\item
  \texttt{dashboard-preview.png} {[}required{]}: screenshot or exported
  preview of the affected dashboard area. This is evidence, not a
  designed product --- a plain screenshot is fine.
\item
  \texttt{reproducibility-note.md} {[}required{]} {[}brief --- 1-3
  focused bullets or sentences are enough{]}: how the dashboard was run
  or rendered in Codespaces. One short paragraph is enough.
\item
  \texttt{ai-use-note.md} {[}required{]}: what AI helped with and how
  you checked it, or a sentence saying AI was not used.
\end{itemize}

\section{Submission Route}\label{submission-route-5}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the required files.

Canvas applies a 7-point rubric. This assignment is part of the
best-4-of-6 set for Assignments 4-9:

needed.

\chapter{Assignment 8: Scientific
Communication}\label{assignment-8-scientific-communication}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-10}

This assignment turns one result from your analysis or dashboard into a
short audience-specific communication product. You will create a small
slide deck or slide outline, write a plain-language summary, cite the
data source, and use peer feedback to improve the claim.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-9}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Translate one technical result into a clear audience-facing takeaway.
\item
  Build a short Quarto revealjs deck or structured slide outline.
\item
  Use one figure or table from prior work as evidence.
\item
  State a limitation and source note beside the claim.
\item
  Give and apply peer feedback on clarity, evidence, and KT value.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-9}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Week 9 page: \texttt{weeks/week09-science-communication/index.qmd}
\item
  Peer feedback page:
  \texttt{weeks/week09-science-communication/comm04-peer-feedback.qmd}
\item
  Renderable slide starter in your personal workspace:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment08-communication/starter-slides.qmd}
\item
  Slide-outline starter in your personal workspace:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment08-communication/slide-outline-template.qmd}
\item
  Peer-feedback template in your personal workspace:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment08-communication/peer-feedback-template.md}
\item
  Prior work from Assignment 5, 6, or 7
\item
  Submission folder:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment08-communication/submission/}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-7}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Create or use the folder
  \texttt{assignments/assignment08-communication/submission/}.
\item
  Choose one figure, table, or dashboard finding from prior work.
\item
  Define the audience in one sentence.
\item
  Write one plain-language takeaway that a non-technical reader can
  understand.
\item
  Create either \texttt{slides.qmd} as a revealjs deck or
  \texttt{slide-outline.qmd} as a structured slide outline. Copy the
  corresponding starter from
  \texttt{assignments/assignment08-communication/} in your personal
  workspace into its \texttt{submission/} folder and rename it before
  editing. Do not edit the original starter.
\item
  Include 3-5 slides or slide sections: context, evidence, takeaway,
  limitation, and next step.
\item
  Add one source note or citation for the dataset.
\item
  Complete the in-studio partner review early enough to make at least
  one A8 revision before submission. Also complete the Canvas Discussion
  exchange by the standard syllabus deadline --- Monday after the Week 9
  class, 4:00 PM. Canvas feedback may inform the presentation deck or
  final-portfolio revisions; it is not the feedback source required
  before the initial A8 submission.
\item
  Revise at least one sentence or slide based on feedback.
\item
  Document any AI assistance used for simplification, slide wording, or
  feedback synthesis.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-7}

AI may help simplify jargon, suggest slide structure, or tighten
wording. You must verify that the simplified text still matches the
evidence, citation, and limitation. AI must not invent findings,
sources, or audience needs. Include \texttt{ai-use-note.md}.

\section{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-7}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The deck or outline must render in GitHub Codespaces.
\item
  Any figure or table must be traceable to prior code or a submitted
  output.
\item
  Source notes and citations must be visible.
\item
  Paths must be relative.
\item
  Peer feedback must be specific enough to act on.
\end{itemize}

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-7}

Place all submission files in:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{assignments/assignment08{-}communication/submission/}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Submit these files:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{slides.qmd} or \texttt{slide-outline.qmd} {[}required{]}:
  source file for the communication product --- submit one of the two.
\item
  \texttt{slides.html} or \texttt{slide-outline.html} {[}required{]}:
  rendered output. Commit this required HTML with its source.
\item
  \texttt{plain-language-summary.md} {[}required{]}: 150-250 word
  audience-facing summary.
\item
  \texttt{peer-feedback.md} {[}required{]}: feedback received and one
  revision made. Expected length: the completed feedback form plus one
  or two sentences describing the revision you made --- do not write an
  essay.
\item
  \texttt{source-note.md} {[}required{]} {[}brief --- 1-3 focused
  bullets or sentences are enough{]}: dataset/source note and citation
  information.
\item
  \texttt{ai-use-note.md} {[}required{]}: what AI helped with and how
  you checked it, or a sentence saying AI was not used.
\end{itemize}

\section{Submission Route}\label{submission-route-6}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the required files.

\section{Grading Checklist}\label{grading-checklist-7}

Canvas applies a 7-point rubric. This assignment is part of the
best-4-of-6 set for Assignments 4-9. The full revealjs deck and the
structured slide outline are graded identically --- choosing the outline
does not lower your possible score.

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lr@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Criterion & Points \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Audience and KT purpose are specific & 1 \\
Slide deck or outline has a clear 3-5 part story & 1.25 \\
Figure/table evidence is traceable and accurately described & 1 \\
Plain-language summary is clear and avoids overclaiming & 1 \\
Source note and limitation are visible & 1 \\
Peer feedback is specific and revision is documented & 1 \\
AI-use note and render/sync requirements are complete & 0.75 \\
\end{longtable}

\section{Definition of Done}\label{definition-of-done-16}

You are done when a peer can read the deck or outline, identify the main
takeaway, see the evidence and limitation, and understand what changed
after feedback.

If you submit an outline for A8, it receives the same A8 score as a
deck. The narrated presentation deck is a later stage and requires
\texttt{slides.qmd}, so convert the revised A8 story into a renderable
deck for the portfolio rather than treating the A8 outline as final
slides.

\chapter{Assignment 9: Reproducible
Report}\label{assignment-9-reproducible-report}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-11}

This assignment expands a project finding into a short reproducible
Quarto report. The report should regenerate a table and figure from
code, cite the data source, state limitations, and prepare the work for
M3 Project Update review. Write the main report for a non-technical
decision-maker audience; technical detail such as code choices and
sensitivity notes belongs in a technical appendix.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-10}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Write a Quarto report that combines narrative, code, tables, figures,
  and citations.
\item
  Generate at least one table and one figure from code.
\item
  Use BibTeX citations and a visible source note.
\item
  Include limitations and a grounding audit for claims.
\item
  Prepare a preview or publishing check suitable for M3 review.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-10}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Week 10 page: \texttt{weeks/week10-reporting-publishing/index.qmd}
\item
  Report template in your personal workspace:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment09-reporting/report-template.qmd}
\item
  Citations how-to: \hyperref[citations-and-grounding]{Week 10 citations
  and grounding page} --- start here if you have not used
  \texttt{references.bib} before
\item
  Bibliography starter in your personal workspace:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment09-reporting/references.bib}
\item
  M3 connection: \texttt{milestones/m3-project-update/README.md}
\item
  Publishing checklist template:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment09-reporting/publishing-checklist-template.md}
\item
  Grounding audit template:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment09-reporting/grounding-audit-template.md}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-8}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Create or use the folder
  \texttt{assignments/assignment09-reporting/submission/}.
\item
  Copy \texttt{report-template.qmd} and \texttt{references.bib} from
  \texttt{assignments/assignment09-reporting/} into its
  \texttt{submission/} folder. Rename the report copy
  \texttt{report.qmd}, then edit the copies rather than the starters.
\item
  Define one descriptive project question. If your project dataset is
  not ready, use the NHANES classroom dataset and explain that this is a
  practice report.
\item
  Load the data with a relative path.
\item
  Generate at least one table and one figure from code.
\item
  Add citations using a local \texttt{references.bib} file or the course
  bibliography workflow.
\item
  Write a limitations section that avoids causal overclaiming.
\item
  Complete a grounding audit: each claim should point to code output, a
  citation, or a stated limitation.
\item
  Complete a publishing check: GitHub Pages preview if available, or a
  private rendered HTML preview in the repository.
\item
  Write an M3 update note explaining what is ready for peer review and
  what feedback would be useful.
\item
  Before committing, search your submission files for absolute or local
  paths (for example \texttt{C:/} or \texttt{/Users/}) and replace any
  you find with relative paths.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-8}

AI may help outline the report, debug Quarto, improve wording, or
identify unclear claims. You must verify every generated number,
citation, and source claim against the rendered output and bibliography.
Include \texttt{ai-use-note.md}.

\section{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-8}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{report.qmd} must render in GitHub Codespaces.
\item
  Tables and figures must be generated by code.
\item
  Paths must be relative.
\item
  Bibliography entries must resolve.
\item
  Publishing preview must not require private local files.
\item
  The report must complement the dashboard-style product rather than
  merely duplicate it.
\end{itemize}

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-8}

Place all submission files in:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{assignments/assignment09{-}reporting/submission/}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Submit these files:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{report.qmd}: rerunnable Quarto report.
\item
  \texttt{report.html}: rendered report committed with its required
  source.
\item
  \texttt{references.bib}: bibliography entries used by the report.
\item
  \texttt{outputs/}: generated table or figure files if the report
  writes external outputs.
\item
  \texttt{publishing-checklist.md}: GitHub Pages or private preview
  check.
\item
  \texttt{grounding-audit.md}: claim-by-claim check against code output,
  citation, or limitation.
\item
  \texttt{m3-update-note.md}: what is ready for M3 review and what
  feedback is requested. At minimum, name one part of the report that is
  ready for review and ask one or two specific feedback questions ---
  for example, ``Is the cohort definition clear?'' or ``Does the
  limitations section cover the missing-data issue?'' The
  \hyperref[assignment-9-and-m3]{Week 10 A9/M3 page} lists more example
  questions.
\item
  \texttt{ai-use-note.md}: what AI helped with and how you checked it,
  or a sentence saying AI was not used.
\end{itemize}

\section{Submission Route}\label{submission-route-7}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the required files.

\section{Definition of Done}\label{definition-of-done-17}

You are done when \texttt{report.qmd} renders to \texttt{report.html},

\chapter{Milestones}\label{milestones}

\chapter{Milestones}\label{milestones-1}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, toptitle=1mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, opacitybacktitle=0.6, leftrule=.75mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-tip-color}{\faLightbulb}\hspace{0.5em}{Worked example}, bottomrule=.15mm, colframe=quarto-callout-tip-color-frame, arc=.35mm, left=2mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-tip-color!10!white, opacityback=0, coltitle=black]

worked example} --- one complete illustrative project from M0 to the
Final Portfolio.

\end{tcolorbox}

Milestones move the term project from team setup to final portfolio.
They are short checkpoints, not separate side projects. Each one should
make the next stage easier to complete and easier for another person to
review.

\section{Submission Contract}\label{submission-contract}

For every milestone:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  commit and sync the required files before submitting
\item
  submit the GitHub repository link on Canvas
\item
  include the latest commit hash in the Canvas comment box
\item
  use relative paths
\item
  document any package or data access requirements
\item
  disclose and audit AI use
\end{itemize}

\section{Project Points and Score
Ownership}\label{project-points-and-score-ownership}

Project scores use direct points that match the course weights: M1--M4
are 5 points each, the Presentation is 10 points, and the Final
Portfolio is 35 points. Staff record a score directly from zero to the
stated maximum using the existing checklist as the evidence guide; there
is no separate performance-band conversion, contribution multiplier, or
adjustment formula.

Shared project artifacts receive one group score. Only a component that
a brief already identifies as individually authored receives an
individual score. The current M1--M4, Presentation, and Final Portfolio
deliverables are shared project artifacts, so each currently receives
one group score.

\section{Reproducibility
Expectations}\label{reproducibility-expectations}

Milestone work should be reviewable in GitHub Codespaces or a documented
local setup. Keep data access clear, use relative paths, and avoid
manual edits to generated outputs unless the edit is explicitly
documented.

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-9}

AI tools may help with drafting, code review, debugging, and clarity
checks. Students remain responsible for correctness. Each milestone
includes an AI-use note so reviewers can see what was AI-assisted and
what was verified by the team.

\section{Definition Of Done}\label{definition-of-done-18}

A milestone is ready when the required files are present, the repository
is synced, the Canvas submission points to the correct commit, and
another student could understand what changed without private
explanation.

\chapter{M0: Group Formation}\label{m0-group-formation}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, toptitle=1mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, opacitybacktitle=0.6, leftrule=.75mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-tip-color}{\faLightbulb}\hspace{0.5em}{See a worked example}, bottomrule=.15mm, colframe=quarto-callout-tip-color-frame, arc=.35mm, left=2mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-tip-color!10!white, opacityback=0, coltitle=black]

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-12}

Project groups are 3--4 students. M0 makes sure every project team has a
working collaboration setup before analytic work begins. By the end of
this milestone, each group should know who is on the team, where the
shared repository lives, how communication will happen, and how the team
will handle reproducibility and AI use.

Your shared repository starts as a fork of the group project template
linked from Canvas --- do not build it as a blank repository. The fork
steps are walked through on the
\hyperref[setting-up-your-group-repository]{Week 4 group repository
page}.

\section{Due Timing}\label{due-timing-1}

Submit by the Week 4 deadline posted in Canvas.

milestone is meant to unblock the project work that follows.

\end{tcolorbox}

  \texttt{ai-use-note.md} {[}required{]}
\end{itemize}

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-9}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the files above. In the Canvas comment box, include the latest
commit hash for the submission.

\section{File Guidance}\label{file-guidance}

Every bullet below is a required field, not an example. One short line
per field is enough.

\texttt{group-formation.md} should include:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  group name
\item
  student names
\item
  GitHub usernames
\item
  one-sentence project area or dataset interest
\item

\texttt{repo-access-evidence.md} should include:

\begin{itemize}

\item

\item

\item

\section{Reproducibility
Expectations}\label{reproducibility-expectations-1}

Use relative paths and keep project files inside the shared repository.
If a group member needs a package, script, or external file to reproduce
work, document it in the repository README before the next milestone.

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-10}

AI tools may help draft communication norms or check clarity. The team
remains responsible for verifying repository access, Codespaces setup,
project scope, and all submitted claims.

\section{Grading Checklist}\label{grading-checklist-9}

This milestone is marked complete when:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  all group members are listed with GitHub usernames
\item
  shared repository access is documented
\item

\item
  Codespaces or local setup has been tested
\item
  AI-use expectations are acknowledged
\item
  files are committed, synced, and linked through Canvas
\end{itemize}

\section{Definition Of Done}\label{definition-of-done-19}

M0 is done when every group member can reach the shared repository,
knows the team communication plan, and can explain how the project will
be kept reproducible from this point forward.

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-13}

M1 turns a broad project idea into a scoped, feasible, and ethically
grounded analytic plan. The proposal should make the audience, data
source, question, reproducibility plan, and first visualization
direction clear enough for feedback.

\section{Due Timing}\label{due-timing-2}

Submit by the Week 6 deadline posted in Canvas. This milestone prepares
the group for Week 7 preliminary analysis and Week 8 dashboard
prototyping.

substantively complete, internally consistent proposal can earn full

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-10}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the files above. In the Canvas comment box, include the latest

\section{Proposal Guidance}\label{proposal-guidance}

Every field below is {[}required{]} unless it carries its own tag.

\texttt{proposal.md} should include:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  project title
\item
  group members
\item
  audience and KT purpose
\item
  one primary question
\item
  one secondary question
\item
  expected data source
\item
  planned analytic output
\item
  known limitations
\end{itemize}

KT purpose means the practical communication goal of your project: who
needs to understand the result, what decision or conversation it
supports, and what caution they need.

The secondary question is not the primary question in different words.
It should narrow or extend the primary one. For example, primary: ``How
does average systolic blood pressure differ across income groups in
NHANES adults?'' Secondary: ``Does that difference look similar for men
and women?''

\texttt{data-intake-card.md} should summarize:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  data source and steward
\item
  access method
\item
  file format
\item
  unit of analysis
\item
  key variables
\item
  privacy and stewardship considerations
\item
  Indigenous Data Sovereignty or CARE/OCAP awareness {[}when relevant
  --- required if your data involve Indigenous Peoples, communities, or
  services{]}
\end{itemize}

CARE/OCAP are principles and frameworks for responsible governance of
Indigenous data. If your dataset includes Indigenous identifiers,
communities, territories, or services, link back to the
\hyperref[health-data-ethics]{Week 1 guidance} before proceeding.

\texttt{initial-visual-plan.md} should include:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  one planned figure
\item
  what the figure should help the audience decide or understand
\item
  one risk of misinterpretation
\end{itemize}

\texttt{reproducibility-plan.md} should include:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  expected repository folders
\item
  data access plan
\item
  package/dependency notes
\item
  how another student could rerun the work
\end{itemize}

\section{Reproducibility
Expectations}\label{reproducibility-expectations-2}

Use relative paths. Do not commit restricted, private, or identifiable
data. If public data are too large for GitHub, commit a small sample or
documented access script and explain how the full file is obtained.

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-11}

AI may help brainstorm wording, search terms, or code structure.
Students must verify data-source claims, privacy considerations,
citations, and feasibility. Include an \texttt{ai-use-note.md}
describing what AI helped with and what was checked manually.

\section{Full-Credit Preparation
Checklist}\label{full-credit-preparation-checklist}

A substantively complete submission should show:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the question is specific and feasible
\item
  the audience and KT purpose are named
\item
  data provenance and access are documented
\item
  privacy, stewardship, and equity risks are acknowledged
\item
  at least one visualization direction is described
\item
  reproducibility plan is concrete
\item
  AI use is disclosed and audited
\end{itemize}

\section{Definition Of Done}\label{definition-of-done-20}

M1 is done when another group can read the proposal and understand what
you will analyze, why it matters, what data you will use, and how the
work will be rerun.

\chapter{M2: Preliminary Analysis}\label{m2-preliminary-analysis}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, toptitle=1mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, opacitybacktitle=0.6, leftrule=.75mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-tip-color}{\faLightbulb}\hspace{0.5em}{See a worked example}, bottomrule=.15mm, colframe=quarto-callout-tip-color-frame, arc=.35mm, left=2mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-tip-color!10!white, opacityback=0, coltitle=black]

\end{tcolorbox}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-14}

M2 checks that the project can move from proposal to evidence. The
milestone should show a documented cohort or analytic sample, a
missingness check, a descriptive summary, and a careful interpretation
that avoids overclaiming.

\section{Due Timing}\label{due-timing-3}

Submit by the Week 7 deadline posted in Canvas. This milestone prepares
the group for Week 8 dashboard prototyping and Week 9 communication
work.

\section{Points and Score Ownership}\label{points-and-score-ownership-1}

M2 is worth \textbf{5 course points} and receives one group score. The
checklist below guides the direct 0--5 score; no contribution multiplier
or performance-band conversion is used.

Criterion & Points \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Cohort definition and inclusion/exclusion decisions & 1 \\
Missingness check and Table 1-style descriptive evidence & 1 \\
Reproducible source, render, and code-generated outputs & 1 \\
Methods note, interpretation, and claim boundary & 1 \\
AI-use audit and complete, synced submission evidence & 1 \\
\textbf{Total} & \textbf{5} \\
\end{longtable}

These are broad judgments, not collections of micro-deductions. A clear,
rerunnable descriptive analysis can earn full credit without advanced
methods or research-grade polish.

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-11}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the files above. In the Canvas comment box, include the latest
commit hash and the folder path for the milestone.

\section{Analysis Requirements}\label{analysis-requirements}

Your preliminary analysis should include:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  a clear analytic sample or cohort definition
\item
  a short explanation of inclusion and exclusion decisions
\item
  a missingness check for key variables
\item
  a Table 1-style descriptive summary
\item
  at least one figure or table generated by code
\item
  a methods note that states what the summary can and cannot claim
\end{itemize}

``Table 1-style'' is health-research shorthand for the first table of a
paper: one row per characteristic of your analytic sample (for example
age, sex, income group), with counts, percentages, and means or medians.
Follow the \hyperref[worked-example-eda-and-table-1]{Week 7 worked
example} for the expected format.

Your group may build on one member's Assignment 6 work. If you do, add
one attribution sentence to \texttt{methods-note.md}, for example: ``The
preliminary analysis builds on Jordan's A6 \texttt{eda-note.qmd}.''
Export the missingness table by code as
\texttt{missingness-summary.csv}; this is an M2-only export and is not
an additional A6 submission file.

\section{Reproducibility
Expectations}\label{reproducibility-expectations-3}

The \texttt{.qmd} file must render in GitHub Codespaces or a documented
local environment. Use relative paths, document packages, and avoid
manual edits to generated tables or figures.

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-12}

AI may help draft code, suggest summaries, or review language.

In your \texttt{ai-use-note.md}, name at least three checks you
performed, such as checking row counts, variable definitions,
missingness, grouping labels, and whether AI changed the meaning of the
result. The \hyperref[ai-safety-the-audit-checklist-1]{Week 3 AI audit
checklist} and the
\hyperref[four-audit-categories-for-ai-drafted-eda]{Week 7 audit
categories} list more checks to choose from.

\section{Full-Credit Preparation
Checklist}\label{full-credit-preparation-checklist-1}

A substantively complete submission should show:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  cohort definition is explicit
\item
  missingness is reported
\item
  Table 1-style output includes counts and descriptive statistics
\item
  code-generated output is reproducible
\item
  interpretation is descriptive and non-causal unless justified
\item
  AI-generated help is disclosed and audited
\item
  files are committed, synced, and submitted through Canvas
\end{itemize}

\section{Definition Of Done}\label{definition-of-done-21}

M2 is done when a peer can rerun the analysis, see the same descriptive
outputs, and understand the limits of the claims being made.

Expectations}\label{reproducibility-expectations-4}

environment. Relative paths are required. Generated figures and tables
should come from code, not manual copying.

\item
  dashboard-style product is visible or clearly previewed
\item
  feedback request is specific
\item

\item

\end{itemize}

\section{Definition Of Done}\label{definition-of-done-22}

report, review the dashboard preview, and provide useful feedback
without guessing what the project is trying to do.

\section{Review Requirements}\label{review-requirements}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  reviewed project title
\item
  two strengths
\item
  three prioritized revision suggestions
\item
  one question for the group
\item
  one issue that would block final reproducibility if unfixed
\item
  a short reviewer reflection answering: what changed in your own
  thinking after reviewing this project; which limitation, bias, or
  stewardship issue deserves the most attention; and one review habit
  you will apply to your own project
\end{itemize}

The reflection belongs at the end of \texttt{peer-review.md}; do not
create a separate \texttt{reviewer-reflection.md} file.

\texttt{reproducibility-check.md} should document:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  whether the report rendered
\item
  whether paths were relative
\item
  whether data access was clear
\item
  whether figures and tables were generated from code
\item
  any error messages or setup blockers
\end{itemize}

\texttt{auditability-check.md} should document:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  whether data provenance is clear
\item
  whether AI use is disclosed
\item
  whether claims are supported by evidence
\item
  whether limitations are stated
\end{itemize}

\texttt{kt-feedback.md} should focus on:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  audience fit
\item
  plain-language clarity
\item
  visual clarity
\item
  actionability
\item
  privacy-safe communication
\end{itemize}

\section{Reproducibility
Expectations}\label{reproducibility-expectations-5}

Run the reviewed project in GitHub Codespaces when possible. If a full
rerun is not possible, document exactly where the process stopped and
what evidence you reviewed instead.

If you cannot rerun the reviewed project in Codespaces, that is
acceptable only if you document what you tried, where it failed, and
what information the project owner would need to fix it. A documented
failed rerun does not block completing M4 --- an undocumented one does.

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-14}

AI may help organize feedback or identify unclear writing. Reviewers
must independently inspect the files, outputs, and claims.

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, toptitle=1mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, opacitybacktitle=0.6, leftrule=.75mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-warning-color}{\faExclamationTriangle}\hspace{0.5em}{Do not let AI invent evidence}, bottomrule=.15mm, colframe=quarto-callout-warning-color-frame, arc=.35mm, left=2mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-warning-color!10!white, opacityback=0, coltitle=black]

Do not use AI to invent results or infer evidence that is not in the
reviewed repository. Every claim in your review must point to something
you actually saw in the files.

\end{tcolorbox}

\section{Full-Credit Preparation
Checklist}\label{full-credit-preparation-checklist-3}

A substantively complete submission should show:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  feedback is specific and actionable
\item
  reproducibility check is documented
\item
  auditability and KT feedback are included
\item
  at least one blocking risk is named or the absence of blockers is
  justified
\item
  AI use is disclosed
\item
  files are committed, synced, and submitted through Canvas
\end{itemize}

\section{Definition Of Done}\label{definition-of-done-23}

M4 is done when the reviewed group can use your comments to improve the
final portfolio without needing a follow-up meeting to understand the
feedback.

\chapter{Presentation (Final Portfolio
Component)}\label{presentation-final-portfolio-component}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, toptitle=1mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, opacitybacktitle=0.6, leftrule=.75mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-tip-color}{\faLightbulb}\hspace{0.5em}{See a worked example}, bottomrule=.15mm, colframe=quarto-callout-tip-color-frame, arc=.35mm, left=2mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-tip-color!10!white, opacityback=0, coltitle=black]

The \href{../example-nhanes/m5.qmd}{the Presentation submission in the
NHANES worked example} shows one illustrative version of this milestone.
Use it for structure and depth --- your project must be your own.

\end{tcolorbox}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-17}

The presentation is the knowledge-translation slide version of the final
project, submitted as a \textbf{narrated 5-7 slide deck} and scored as
the Presentation component (10 points) of the
\href{../final-portfolio/README.md}{Final Portfolio}. It should explain
the audience, question, data, one or two main findings, limitations, and
the practical meaning of the work without overclaiming.

\section{Due Timing}\label{due-timing-6}

The narrated deck is submitted online with the Final Portfolio on
\textbf{Dec 11}; there is no in-class presentation session. Record
narration over your 5-7 slides (a narrated
\texttt{slides.html}/\texttt{slides.pdf} export, or a short screen
recording) and commit it with the slide source.

\section{Points and Score Ownership}\label{points-and-score-ownership-4}

The presentation is worth \textbf{10 course points} and receives one
group score. The checklist below guides the direct 0--10 score; no
contribution multiplier or performance-band conversion is used.

The ten points are allocated across five broad criteria:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lr@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Criterion & Points \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Audience, purpose, and story & 2 \\
Source, evidence, and interpretation & 2 \\
Limitations, privacy, and stewardship & 2 \\
Render, readability, narration, and pacing & 2 \\
Feedback, revision, reproducibility, and AI evidence & 2 \\
\textbf{Total} & \textbf{10} \\
\end{longtable}

These categories do not reward decorative polish or a particular
presentation tool. A plain, readable, evidence-based presentation can
earn full credit. One underlying problem is assessed under the category
that most directly owns it rather than being automatically deducted in
several categories.

\section{Required Deliverables}\label{required-deliverables-5}

Create or update:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{milestones/m5{-}presentation/submission/}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Include these files. All are required; tags follow
\hyperref[how-to-read-submission-lists]{How To Read Submission Lists}.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{slides.qmd} {[}required{]}
\item
  \texttt{slides.html} or \texttt{slides.pdf} {[}required{]} --- commit
  this explicitly required rendered file with \texttt{slides.qmd}
\item
  narrated recording {[}required{]} --- a narrated export of the slides
  or a short screen recording; if narration is embedded in
  \texttt{slides.html}/\texttt{slides.pdf}, note that in
  \texttt{presentation-checklist.md}
\item
  \texttt{source-and-limitations-note.md} {[}required{]}
\item
  \texttt{presentation-checklist.md} {[}required{]}
\item
  \texttt{peer-feedback-received.md} {[}required{]} {[}brief --- 1-3
  focused bullets or sentences are enough{]}
\item
  \texttt{ai-use-note.md} {[}required{]}
\end{itemize}

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-14}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the files above. In the Canvas comment box, include the latest
commit hash, the slide path, and any rendered slide link.

\section{Slide Requirements}\label{slide-requirements}

The presentation requires a renderable \texttt{slides.qmd}. If your A8
submission used the equally valid outline pathway, convert that revised
story into the Week 9 slide starter now; this is the intended
A8-to-presentation progression, not a new communication task.

Use 5 to 7 slides:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Title, team, audience, and KT purpose
\item
  Question and why it matters
\item
  Data source, analytic sample, and stewardship note
\item
  Main figure or table
\item
  Interpretation and limitation
\item
  Practical implication or next step
\item
  Backup or Q\&A slide {[}optional --- include only if relevant to your
  project; a 5- or 6-slide deck with no backup slide loses nothing{]}
\end{enumerate}

\section{Source And Limitations Note}\label{source-and-limitations-note}

\texttt{source-and-limitations-note.md} should include:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  data source and citation
\item
  key inclusion or exclusion choices
\item
  whether results are descriptive, predictive, or causal
\item
  one privacy or stewardship caution
\item
  one limitation that should be stated aloud
\end{itemize}

\section{Peer Feedback Received}\label{peer-feedback-received}

\texttt{peer-feedback-received.md} should summarize the feedback your
group received, identify the source of the feedback, and state what you
changed or chose not to change before finalizing the narrated deck. The
main source is the M4 peer review your group received; peer feedback
from the Week 9 exchange counts too if you used it. You are summarizing
existing feedback, not writing a new response document.

\section{Reproducibility
Expectations}\label{reproducibility-expectations-6}

Slides should render in GitHub Codespaces or a documented local
environment. Figures and tables should come from the project repository
whenever possible. Screenshots may be used for dashboards, but the
source page or app path must be documented.

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-15}

AI may help improve slide wording, draft speaker notes, or identify
jargon. Students must verify all numbers, citations, screenshots, and
claims. The \texttt{ai-use-note.md} should state what AI helped with and
what was checked manually.

\section{Presentation Preparation
Checklist}\label{presentation-preparation-checklist}

The bullets below unpack the five scored categories above; they are
preparation checks, not additional scored rows.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  audience and KT purpose are clear
\item
  question is focused
\item
  data source and limitations are transparent
\item
  main visual is readable on screen
\item
  interpretation is accurate and non-causal unless justified
\item
  privacy and stewardship risks are acknowledged
\item
  narration fits the target length
\item
  likely questions are anticipated in the deck or a backup slide
\end{itemize}

\section{Definition Of Done}\label{definition-of-done-24}

The presentation is done when the narrated slides are committed,
rendered, and submitted with the Final Portfolio, and play without
relying on local-only files.

\chapter{Final Portfolio}\label{final-portfolio}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, toptitle=1mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, opacitybacktitle=0.6, leftrule=.75mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-tip-color}{\faLightbulb}\hspace{0.5em}{See a worked example}, bottomrule=.15mm, colframe=quarto-callout-tip-color-frame, arc=.35mm, left=2mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-tip-color!10!white, opacityback=0, coltitle=black]

The \href{../example-nhanes/final.qmd}{the Final Portfolio submission in
the NHANES worked example} shows one illustrative version of this
milestone. Use it for structure and depth --- your project must be your
own.

\end{tcolorbox}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-18}

The final portfolio is the complete project record: report, code, data
documentation, dashboard-style KT product, presentation materials,
reproducibility evidence, and AI-use audit. It should let another person
understand what was done, why it matters, and how to rerun or inspect
the work.

\section{Due Timing}\label{due-timing-7}

Submit by the final portfolio deadline posted in Canvas. The syllabus
deadline is the course final submission deadline unless Canvas states a
more specific time.

\section{Points and Score Ownership}\label{points-and-score-ownership-5}

The Final Portfolio is worth \textbf{35 course points} and receives one
group score. The existing portfolio and reproducibility checklists guide
the direct 0--35 score; no contribution multiplier, complicated
adjustment formula, or performance-band conversion is used.

The 35 points are allocated across five broad categories:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.4286}}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5714}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Criterion
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft
Points
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Final report and evidence & 10 \\
Reproducibility and traceability & 10 \\
KT product and presentation alignment & 5 \\
Provenance, stewardship, and limitations & 5 \\
README, repository access, AI audit, risk list, and submission record &
5 \\
\textbf{Total} & \textbf{35} \\
\end{longtable}

These categories are broad judgments, not containers for dozens of tiny
deductions. The portfolio rewards coherent evidence and a workflow
another person can understand and inspect. It does not reward
statistical sophistication, public deployment, Shiny,
publication-quality prose, or decorative polish. One underlying problem
is assessed under the category that most directly owns it unless it
causes genuinely separate required failures.

The \textbf{narrated presentation deck} is a required part of the
portfolio and is scored separately as the \textbf{Presentation}
component (10 points); see
\hyperref[presentation-final-portfolio-component]{Presentation}. It is
submitted online with the portfolio on Dec 11 --- there is no in-class
presentation.

\section{Required Deliverables}\label{required-deliverables-6}

Create or update:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{milestones/final{-}portfolio/submission/}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Include these files:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{final-portfolio-checklist.md}
\item
  \texttt{repository-access-note.md}
\item
  \texttt{final-risk-list.md}
\item
  \texttt{ai-use-note.md}
\end{itemize}

Your project repository should also include:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  final report source and rendered output
\item
  dashboard-style KT product or static preview
\item
  data provenance and stewardship documentation
\item
  reproducibility instructions
\item
  narrated presentation slide deck (5-7 slides, recorded narration)
\item
  clear README for the whole project
\end{itemize}

A live dashboard is not required unless the assignment says so. A static
preview, screenshot, or rendered HTML page is acceptable when it clearly
shows the KT product and passes the privacy check.

The three KT-product options, from heaviest to lightest:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Live dashboard} --- an interactive page or app a reviewer can
  open and click (for example, a demo-only Shiny app). Never required.
\item
  \textbf{Static preview} --- a rendered, non-interactive HTML page
  committed to the repository that shows the KT product's current state.
\item
  \textbf{Screenshot} --- an image file of the product committed to the
  repository, with a note saying which file or app it shows.
\end{itemize}

Any of the three is acceptable for the final portfolio.

\section{Repository Access Note}\label{repository-access-note}

Keep \texttt{repository-access-note.md} short. It must state:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  repository URL and whether it is public or private;
\item
  latest submitted commit hash;
\item
  confirmation that course staff can open the repository;
\item
  if private, who granted staff access and the date access was checked;
\item
  if public, confirmation that the safe-publishing check was completed;
\item
  relative paths to the final report and dashboard/KT product.
\end{itemize}

This note records access facts only; it is not a new reflection or
project summary.

Revising earlier work is allowed and encouraged: the final portfolio is
the right place to apply M4 feedback and improve assignments or
milestone files you already submitted. Only the files listed above are
prescribed. Beyond them, organize the repository however serves your
project best, as long as the README explains the structure.

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-15}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing all final materials. In the Canvas comment box, include:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  latest commit hash
\item
  final report path
\item
  dashboard or KT product path
\item
  final portfolio checklist path
\item
  rendered public link if the repository is public and safe to publish
\end{itemize}

\section{Public And Private Repository
Guidance}\label{public-and-private-repository-guidance}

Use a public repository only when the data and outputs are allowed to be
public. Do not publish restricted, private, identifiable, or culturally
sensitive data. If the repository must stay private, keep it private,
grant course staff access, and submit the private repository link on
Canvas.

Public repositories should avoid raw restricted data and should use
documented public data, simulated data, or aggregate outputs that are
safe to share. Private repositories still require clear documentation
and reproducibility instructions for course staff. See
\href{repository-sharing-guidance.md}{Repository Sharing Guidance}
before changing repository visibility.

\section{Reproducibility Checklist}\label{reproducibility-checklist}

Use the \href{final-portfolio-checklist.md}{Final Portfolio Checklist}
to confirm:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  repository opens from a fresh clone or Codespace
\item
  README explains the project, audience, and file structure
\item
  all paths are relative
\item
  required packages are documented
\item
  report renders from source
\item
  every rendered artifact explicitly required for the portfolio is
  committed with its source
\item
  dashboard or KT product can be opened or rerun, or its static preview
  is committed
\item
  generated figures and tables can be traced to code
\item
  data provenance and access rules are documented
\item
  privacy and stewardship risks are addressed
\item
  AI use is disclosed and audited
\item
  final commit is synced before Canvas submission
\end{itemize}

\section{What A Strong Project README Looks
Like}\label{what-a-strong-project-readme-looks-like}

Weak: ``Final project for SPPH 381H. See files.''

Strong:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{\# Income and Blood Pressure in NHANES Adults}

\NormalTok{Audience: community health planners (non{-}technical).}
\NormalTok{Question: how does average systolic blood pressure differ across income groups?}

\NormalTok{{-} report/    — final report source (report.qmd) and rendered report.html}
\NormalTok{{-} dashboard/ — KT product static preview (preview.html) and screenshot}
\NormalTok{{-} data/      — documented access script; no raw restricted data committed}
\NormalTok{{-} milestones/ — milestone submissions, including the final portfolio checklist}

\NormalTok{To rerun: open the repo in Codespaces, then render report/report.qmd.}
\NormalTok{Required packages are listed in reproducibility{-}note.md.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The strong version answers the four reader questions: what the project
is, who it is for, where each artifact lives, and how to rerun it.

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-16}

AI may help polish writing, check code, create revision plans, or test
explanations. Students must verify all outputs, citations, code, numeric
results, and interpretations. The final \texttt{ai-use-note.md} should
describe major AI uses across the project and the checks used to protect
correctness.

\section{Grading Checklist}\label{grading-checklist-10}

The final portfolio is ready for assessment when:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  report is complete and evidence-based
\item
  dashboard-style KT product matches the intended audience
\item
  data provenance and limitations are transparent
\item
  repository is organized and reproducible
\item
  final outputs are committed and synced
\item
  narrated presentation deck aligns with the final project
\item
  AI-use audit is complete
\item
  repository access route is clear
\end{itemize}

\section{Definition Of Done}\label{definition-of-done-25}

The final portfolio is done when course staff can open the submitted
repository, locate the final artifacts, rerun or inspect the work, and
understand the project without asking for missing files.

\chapter{Final Portfolio Checklist}\label{final-portfolio-checklist}

Use this checklist before submitting the final repository link on
Canvas.

\section{Repository Access}\label{repository-access}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item[$\square$]
  Repository link opens for course staff.
\item[$\square$]
  Private repository access has been granted to course staff when
  needed.
\item[$\square$]
  Latest commit is synced to GitHub.
\item[$\square$]
  Canvas submission includes the latest commit hash.
\item[$\square$]
  \texttt{repository-access-note.md} records visibility, staff-access
  status, the checked date, latest commit hash, and final artifact
  paths.
\end{itemize}

\section{Project README}\label{project-readme}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item[$\square$]
  README names the project audience and purpose.
\item[$\square$]
  README explains the folder structure.
\item[$\square$]
  README explains how to render the report.
\item[$\square$]
  README explains how to open or run the KT product.
\item[$\square$]
  README names any required packages or environment setup.
\end{itemize}

\section{Data And Stewardship}\label{data-and-stewardship}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item[$\square$]
  Data source is cited.
\item[$\square$]
  Access method is documented.
\item[$\square$]
  Data restrictions are stated.
\item[$\square$]
  Privacy and stewardship risks are acknowledged.
\item[$\square$]
  Public repository contains only shareable data and outputs.
\end{itemize}

\section{Reproducibility}\label{reproducibility-1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item[$\square$]
  Paths are relative.
\item[$\square$]
  Report renders from source.
\item[$\square$]
  Figures and tables are generated by code.
\item[$\square$]
  Dashboard-style product opens or runs, or a static preview (screenshot
  or rendered HTML page) is committed in its place.
\item[$\square$]
  Dependency notes are current.
\item[$\square$]
  Known failures are recorded in the risk list.
\end{itemize}

\section{Communication}\label{communication}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item[$\square$]
  Final report states the question, data, methods, findings, and
  limitations.
\item[$\square$]
  KT product is matched to the intended audience.
\item[$\square$]
  Presentation materials align with the final findings.
\item[$\square$]
  Claims are descriptive, predictive, or causal only when justified.
\end{itemize}

\section{AI Use}\label{ai-use-1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item[$\square$]
  AI-use note lists major AI-assisted tasks.
\item[$\square$]
  Numeric outputs and citations were verified manually.
\item[$\square$]
  AI-generated code was tested.
\item[$\square$]
  AI-generated prose was reviewed for accuracy and tone.
\end{itemize}

\section{Definition Of Done}\label{definition-of-done-26}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item[$\square$]
  Final repository link submitted on Canvas.
\item[$\square$]
  Commit hash included in Canvas.
\item[$\square$]
  Final report path included in Canvas.
\item[$\square$]
  Dashboard or KT product path included in Canvas.
\end{itemize}

\chapter{Repository Sharing Guidance}\label{repository-sharing-guidance}

Use this guidance when deciding whether the final project repository can
be public.

\section{Public Repository}\label{public-repository}

A public repository is appropriate when:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  data are public and permitted for redistribution, or raw data are not
  committed
\item
  outputs do not reveal private, identifiable, restricted, or culturally
  sensitive information
\item
  data source terms allow public sharing
\item
  the README explains where data came from and how to rerun the project
\end{itemize}

Public repositories are useful for portfolios, but publication is never
more important than privacy, stewardship, or data-use rules.

\section{Private Repository}\label{private-repository}

Use a private repository when:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the data source is restricted
\item
  the dataset includes sensitive or potentially identifiable records
\item
  a data steward has not approved public sharing
\item
  the group is unsure whether public release is allowed
\end{itemize}

For a private repository, grant course staff access and submit the
private GitHub repository link on Canvas. The project still needs
reproducibility instructions for course staff.

Record the chosen route in \texttt{repository-access-note.md} using the
field list in the \hyperref[repository-access-note]{Final Portfolio
brief}. The note lets the grader verify the submitted link and artifact
paths without guessing; it does not require a justification for keeping
the repository private.

\section{Safe Publishing Check}\label{safe-publishing-check}

Before making a repository public, confirm:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  no restricted raw data are committed
\item
  no credentials, tokens, or private URLs are committed
\item
  no individual-level sensitive examples appear in outputs
\item
  figures and tables are aggregate or otherwise safe to share
\item
  README states the data source and sharing limits
\end{itemize}

\part{Worked Example (NHANES)}

\chapter{Worked Example (NHANES)}\label{worked-example-nhanes-1}

\chapter*{Worked Example (NHANES)}\label{worked-example-nhanes-2}
\addcontentsline{toc}{chapter}{Worked Example (NHANES)}

\markboth{Worked Example (NHANES)}{Worked Example (NHANES)}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, toptitle=1mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, opacitybacktitle=0.6, leftrule=.75mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-warning-color}{\faExclamationTriangle}\hspace{0.5em}{Illustrative example --- do not copy}, bottomrule=.15mm, colframe=quarto-callout-warning-color-frame, arc=.35mm, left=2mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-warning-color!10!white, opacityback=0, coltitle=black]

This is \textbf{one} worked example on the class NHANES dataset, written
by a \emph{fictional} group, to show the expected \textbf{structure and
depth} of a good submission. Your project must use \textbf{your own}
question, data, audience, and analysis --- do not copy this text or
these files. This example lives in the public book for learning; graders
assess your original work.

\end{tcolorbox}

To make the milestone briefs concrete, this section shows \textbf{one
complete worked project} --- the fictional \emph{Cedar Equity Lab} group
studying income and BMI patterns in the class NHANES dataset ---
developed all the way from group formation to the final portfolio.

Read it for the \textbf{shape and quality} of good work: how a question
is scoped, how a Table 1 and missingness summary are presented, how
limitations are stated, how AI use is documented, and how a peer review
is written. Then build \textbf{your own} project on \textbf{your own}
question and data.

\emph{File: \texttt{data-intake-card.md}}

\section{Data intake card}\label{data-intake-card}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Dataset:} NHANES Health Equity classroom dataset
\item
  \textbf{Source and steward:} CDC/National Center for Health
  Statistics, National Health and Nutrition Examination Survey
\item
  \textbf{Access:} Prepared CSV committed at
  \texttt{data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}; source documentation at
  \texttt{https://wwwn.cdc.gov/nchs/nhanes/}; checked 2026-10-01
\item
  \textbf{Terms:} Public-use NHANES files; cite CDC/NCHS documentation
\item
  \textbf{Geography and time:} United States; cycles represented from
  1999-2000 through 2017-2018 and 2021-2023
\item
  \textbf{Format and level:} CSV; row-level public-use-derived classroom
  records
\item
  \textbf{Unit of analysis:} One examination participant record within a
  survey cycle
\item
  \textbf{Key variables:} \texttt{Age} in years; \texttt{BMI} in kg/m²;
  \texttt{IncomeGroup} derived from the family income-to-poverty ratio;
  \texttt{Cycle} as the NHANES cycle label
\item
  \textbf{Privacy/security:} Use only aggregate outputs, avoid
  small-cell claims, and do not upload row-level records to external AI
  tools
\item
  \textbf{Stewardship:} Describe group comparisons without stigma and
  keep the prepared, unweighted classroom boundary beside results
\item
  \textbf{Indigenous Data Sovereignty relevance:} No specific Indigenous
  community, service, territory, or identifier is part of the proposed
  comparison. We will reassess CARE/OCAP relevance if the project scope
  changes.
\item
  \textbf{Known limitations:} Population inference requires the correct
  NHANES survey design; the prepared file is not a causal dataset
\item
  \textbf{KT framing:} The data can identify a descriptive pattern worth
  discussing while teaching the audience what the comparison cannot
  establish
\item
  \textbf{Citation:} CDC/NCHS, National Health and Nutrition Examination
  Survey, \texttt{https://wwwn.cdc.gov/nchs/nhanes/}, accessed
  2026-10-01
\end{itemize}

AI could misstate the \texttt{IncomeGroup} cut-points or describe the
group as an individual income measure. We checked the variable creation
in the course case-study build script and will describe it as a derived
category.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\emph{File: \texttt{initial-visual-plan.md}}

\section{Initial visual plan}\label{initial-visual-plan}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Planned figure: a line plot of unweighted mean BMI across NHANES
  cycles, with one line for each income group and visible cycle and BMI
  labels.
\item
  Audience purpose: help briefing staff see whether the small overall
  income-group difference is similar across cycles or concentrated in a
  few periods.
\item
  Misinterpretation risk: connected lines may be read as a causal trend
  or a national estimate. The title and caption will say that the
  summary is descriptive, unweighted, and based on the prepared
  classroom records.
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\emph{File: \texttt{proposal.md}}

\section{Project proposal}\label{project-proposal}

\subsection{Project and team}\label{project-and-team}

\textbf{Title:} Income and BMI Patterns in NHANES Adults

\textbf{Group:} Cedar Equity Lab --- Maya Chen, Jordan Singh, and Alex
Martin

\subsection{Audience and KT purpose}\label{audience-and-kt-purpose}

Our audience is municipal public-health staff preparing an internal
health-equity briefing. The project will give them a simple descriptive
view of income-group differences in the prepared dataset, while keeping
the unweighted and non-causal limits visible. It is intended to support
a discussion about what deserves closer survey-aware analysis, not an
immediate policy decision.

\subsection{Questions}\label{questions}

\textbf{Primary question:} How does mean BMI differ across income groups
among adults age 20-80 in the prepared NHANES Health Equity classroom
dataset?

\textbf{Secondary question:} How does mean BMI vary across available
NHANES cycles for one selected income group?

\subsection{Data and planned output}\label{data-and-planned-output}

We will use the cached CSV derived from CDC/NCHS NHANES public-use
files. The planned outputs are a Table 1-style summary, one
cycle-by-income figure, a short Quarto report, and a small Quarto
dashboard-style page with one editable income group.

\subsection{Known limitations}\label{known-limitations}

The cached file is a prepared classroom dataset. The proposed summaries
are unweighted, will exclude some missing values after reporting them,
and cannot support causal or national-population claims. A full NHANES
analysis would require survey weights, strata, and primary sampling
units.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\emph{File: \texttt{reproducibility-plan.md}}

\section{Reproducibility plan}\label{reproducibility-plan}

Expected folders are \texttt{data/}, \texttt{report/},
\texttt{dashboard/}, and \texttt{milestones/}. The cached
public-use-derived CSV will be committed once under \texttt{data/} and
treated as read-only. R source files will load \texttt{tidyverse} and
\texttt{knitr} explicitly and use paths relative to the project root.

Another student should be able to open the course Codespace, start at
the repository root, and run \texttt{quarto\ render} on the named
source. Generated tables, figures, and HTML files will be regenerated
from code rather than edited by hand. Any added dependency will be
recorded before use.

Feasibility check: the CSV loaded successfully in the course environment
with 105,626 rows and 18 columns. R is the project language; a Python
version is not needed for the proposed questions.

\emph{File: \texttt{methods-note.md}}

\section{Methods note}\label{methods-note}

We used the prepared NHANES Health Equity classroom CSV and restricted
the cohort to ages 20-80, inclusive. We reported missingness for the key
variables before selecting complete records for age, BMI, and income
group. The grouped summary reports N, BMI mean (SD), and age mean (SD)
by \texttt{IncomeGroup}; the figure shows unweighted mean BMI by income
group and cycle. These results describe the prepared classroom records
and cannot support causal or national-population claims.

The preliminary analysis builds on Jordan's A6 \texttt{eda-note.qmd};
the group rechecked the cohort, denominators, exports, and wording
before reuse.

\section{Feedback request}\label{feedback-request-1}

Please focus on these questions:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Correctness:} Do the complete-case counts in the report agree
  with the table and figure?
\item
  \textbf{Interpretation:} Is the difference described as modest and
  descriptive, without implying causality or national representation?
\item
  \textbf{Visual clarity:} Does the cycle figure make the small group
  difference understandable without exaggerating it?
\item
  \textbf{Reproducibility:} Are the root render commands and data path
  clear enough to follow in a fresh Codespace?
\item
  \textbf{Audience fit:} Would a municipal public-health briefing team
  understand what question this result should prompt next?
\end{itemize}

  more important than it is?
